//! The binder's type model: what a Quarry expression is, statically, so a
//! program's arguments and result reach the client as TypeScript types by
//! inference (nothing is annotated, nothing is committed).

use crate::ast::{ScalarType, TypeKind};
use snapback4_core::ir::ArgType;
use snapback4_core::schema::{ColumnType, TextFormat};
use std::collections::BTreeMap;

#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum Ty {
    Unknown,
    Null,
    Bool,
    Int,
    Time,
    Decimal(u8),
    Money(String, u8),
    Text(u32),
    Id,
    Principal,
    Ref(String),
    Bytes,
    Json,
    Image,
    Video,
    File,
    Enum(Vec<String>),
    Cursor,
    Optional(Box<Ty>),
    /// A bounded list of scalars; `max` bounds a loop over it.
    List(Box<Ty>, u32),
    /// One row of a table, present.
    Row(String),
    WriteRow(String),
    /// A bounded page of rows, in index order.
    Page(String, u32),
    /// A shaped page: at most `max` objects.
    Collection(Box<Ty>, u32),
    Object(BTreeMap<String, Ty>),
    /// A projection whose row base carries optional device metadata.
    RowShape(BTreeMap<String, Ty>),
    /// Object-spread fields with row metadata, but ordinary object wildcard order.
    RowObject(BTreeMap<String, Ty>),

}

impl Ty {
    /// A reassigned allocation is bounded by the heap meter, not its first literal's length.
    pub(crate) fn rebound(self) -> Ty {
        match self {
            Ty::Text(_) => Ty::Text(u32::MAX),
            // A known empty value stays empty, even at a loop header.
            Ty::List(_, 0) | Ty::Collection(_, 0) => self,
            Ty::List(item, _) => Ty::List(Box::new(item.rebound()), u32::MAX),
            Ty::Collection(item, _) => Ty::Collection(Box::new(item.rebound()), u32::MAX),
            Ty::Object(fields) => Ty::Object(fields.into_iter().map(|(name, ty)| (name, ty.rebound())).collect()),
            Ty::RowShape(fields) => Ty::RowShape(fields.into_iter().map(|(name, ty)| (name, ty.rebound())).collect()),
            Ty::RowObject(fields) => Ty::RowObject(fields.into_iter().map(|(name, ty)| (name, ty.rebound())).collect()),
            Ty::Optional(inner) => inner.rebound().optional(),
            other => other,
        }
    }

    pub fn optional(self) -> Ty {
        match self {
            Ty::Optional(_) | Ty::Null | Ty::Unknown => self,
            other => Ty::Optional(Box::new(other)),
        }
    }

    pub fn required(&self) -> Ty {
        match self {
            Ty::Optional(inner) => inner.as_ref().clone(),
            other => other.clone(),
        }
    }

    pub fn is_optional(&self) -> bool {
        matches!(self, Ty::Optional(_) | Ty::Null)
    }

    pub fn is_bool(&self) -> bool {
        matches!(self, Ty::Bool)
    }

    pub fn is_numeric(&self) -> bool {
        matches!(self, Ty::Int | Ty::Time | Ty::Decimal(_) | Ty::Money(..))
    }

    pub fn is_identity(&self) -> bool {
        matches!(self, Ty::Id | Ty::Principal | Ty::Ref(_))
    }

    pub fn from_scalar(scalar: &ScalarType) -> Ty {
        let base = match &scalar.kind {
            TypeKind::Cursor => Ty::Cursor,
            TypeKind::Text { max, .. } => Ty::Text(*max),
            TypeKind::Id => Ty::Id,
            TypeKind::Principal => Ty::Principal,
            TypeKind::Int | TypeKind::IntRange { .. } => Ty::Int,
            TypeKind::Decimal { scale } => Ty::Decimal(*scale),
            TypeKind::Money { currency, scale } => Ty::Money(currency.clone(), *scale),
            TypeKind::Time => Ty::Time,
            TypeKind::Bool => Ty::Bool,
            TypeKind::Json { .. } => Ty::Json,
            TypeKind::Bytes { .. } => Ty::Bytes,
            TypeKind::Image { .. } => Ty::Image,
            TypeKind::Video { .. } => Ty::Video,
            TypeKind::File { .. } => Ty::File,
            TypeKind::Enum { variants } => Ty::Enum(variants.clone()),
            TypeKind::Ref { table } => Ty::Ref(table.clone()),
            TypeKind::List { item, max, .. } => Ty::List(Box::new(Ty::from_scalar(item)), *max),
        };
        if scalar.optional { base.optional() } else { base }
    }

    pub fn from_column(kind: &ColumnType) -> Ty {
        match kind {
            ColumnType::Id => Ty::Id,
            ColumnType::Principal => Ty::Principal,
            ColumnType::Text { max } => Ty::Text(*max),
            ColumnType::Bytes { .. } => Ty::Bytes,
            ColumnType::Image { .. } => Ty::Image,
            ColumnType::Video { .. } => Ty::Video,
            ColumnType::File { .. } => Ty::File,
            ColumnType::Int | ColumnType::IntRange { .. } => Ty::Int,
            ColumnType::Enum { variants } => Ty::Enum(variants.clone()),
            ColumnType::Decimal { scale } => Ty::Decimal(*scale),
            ColumnType::Money { currency, scale } => Ty::Money(currency.clone(), *scale),
            ColumnType::Bool => Ty::Bool,
            ColumnType::Time => Ty::Time,
            ColumnType::Ref { table } => Ty::Ref(table.clone()),
            ColumnType::Json { .. } => Ty::Json,
            ColumnType::Optional(inner) => Ty::from_column(inner).optional(),
            ColumnType::List { of, max } | ColumnType::ListRange { of, max, .. } => Ty::List(Box::new(Ty::from_column(of)), *max),
        }
    }

    /// The name a diagnostic prints.
    pub fn name(&self) -> String {
        match self {
            Ty::Unknown => "an unknown value".into(),
            Ty::Null => "null".into(),
            Ty::Bool => "bool".into(),
            Ty::Int => "int".into(),
            Ty::Time => "time".into(),
            Ty::Decimal(scale) => format!("decimal {scale}"),
            Ty::Money(currency, scale) => format!("money {currency} {scale}"),
            Ty::Text(max) => format!("text <={max}"),
            Ty::Id => "id".into(),
            Ty::Principal => "principal".into(),
            Ty::Ref(table) => format!("a {table} reference"),
            Ty::Bytes => "bytes".into(),
            Ty::Json => "json".into(),
//! Immutable source releases with a database-authoritative activation pointer.
use super::*;
use crate::store_owner::Owner;
use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};
#[path = "staging.rs"]
mod staging;
struct Stage(PathBuf);
impl Drop for Stage {fn drop(&mut self){let _=std::fs::remove_dir_all(&self.0);}}

fn directory(options: &Options) -> Result<PathBuf, String> {
    options
        .positionals
        .first()
        .map(PathBuf::from)
        .ok_or_else(|| "a deployment directory is required".into())
}

// @ref LLP 3000.000.000#5.1 — deployment aliases share ownership, control and worker paths.
fn canonical_directory(options: &Options) -> Result<PathBuf, String> {
    let requested = directory(options)?;
    if !requested.exists() {
        snapback4_core::private_fs::directory(&requested).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
    }
    let dir = requested.canonicalize().map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
    snapback4_core::private_fs::directory(&dir).map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
    Ok(dir)
}

// @ref LLP 3000.000.000.000.000#8 — validate before any writable ownership or command lock.
pub(crate) fn existing_deployment(requested: &Path) -> Result<PathBuf, String> {
    let missing = || format!("no current release in {}; run `snapback4 deploy {}` first", requested.display(), requested.display());
    if !requested.join("store.sqlite").is_file() { return Err(missing()); }
    let dir = requested.canonicalize().map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
    inspect_current(&dir)?;
    Ok(dir)
}

// SQLite READONLY can create WAL sidecars. A stopped store's existing owner
// lock makes immutable inspection safe; a live host supplies its own status.
fn inspect_current(dir: &Path) -> Result<u64, String> {
    use rusqlite::{Connection, OpenFlags, OptionalExtension};
    let database = dir.join("store.sqlite").canonicalize().map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
    let missing = || format!("no current release in {}; run `snapback4 deploy {}` first", dir.display(), dir.display());
    let owner = match snapback4_core::private_fs::auxiliary_file(&database,
        &snapback4_core::private_fs::owner_path(&database), false, 0) {
        Ok(owner) => owner,
        Err(error) if error.kind() == std::io::ErrorKind::NotFound => {
            // Relocated/legacy deployments may not yet have a sidecar beside
            // the resolved DB. Establish a complete current source first; only
            // a known deployment may let SQLite create its ordinary WAL files.
            return pointer_current(dir, &database);
        },
        Err(error) => return Err(format!("{}; existing ownership sidecar unavailable: {error}", missing())),
    };
    match owner.try_lock() {
        Ok(()) => {},
        Err(std::fs::TryLockError::WouldBlock) => {
            match crate::release_control::request(dir, crate::release_control::Command::Status {after:0, items_after:None, event:None}) {
                Ok(status) => return status["release"].as_u64().filter(|n|*n>0).ok_or_else(missing),
                Err(error) => {
                    // A killed host's watchdogs retain ownership during their
                    // bounded cleanup. Preserve serve's existing restart wait.
                    if !crate::store_owner::stopped_host(&database) {
                        // A second source directory may alias the same DB. Its
                        // source cannot control this host, but its token still
                        // belongs to the resolved database. Establish current
                        // source before permitting ordinary read-only WAL work.
                        return pointer_current(dir, &database).map_err(|_| format!("{}; {error}",missing()));
                    }
                    let deadline=std::time::Instant::now()+std::time::Duration::from_secs(5);
                    loop {
                        match owner.try_lock() {
                            Ok(()) => break,
                            Err(std::fs::TryLockError::WouldBlock) if std::time::Instant::now()<deadline => std::thread::sleep(std::time::Duration::from_millis(25)),
                            _ => return Err(format!("{}; {error}",missing())),
                        }
                    }
                }
            }
        },
        Err(error) => return Err(error.to_string()),
    }
    if !snapback4_core::private_fs::owner_is_named(&database, &owner) {
        return Err("store ownership changed during release inspection; retry".into());
    }
    #[cfg(unix)]
    let bytes = { use std::os::unix::ffi::OsStrExt; database.as_os_str().as_bytes() };
    #[cfg(not(unix))]
    let bytes = database.to_str().ok_or("invalid store path")?.as_bytes();
    let mut uri = String::from("file:");
    for byte in bytes {
        if byte.is_ascii_alphanumeric() || b"/._-".contains(byte) { uri.push(*byte as char); }
        else { uri.push_str(&format!("%{byte:02X}")); }
    }
    let mut wal = database.as_os_str().to_os_string(); wal.push("-wal"); let wal = PathBuf::from(wal);
    let mut shm = database.as_os_str().to_os_string(); shm.push("-shm"); let shm = PathBuf::from(shm);
    let has_wal = match std::fs::metadata(&wal) {
        Ok(meta) => meta.len() > 0,
        Err(e) if e.kind() == std::io::ErrorKind::NotFound => false,
        Err(e) => return Err(e.to_string()),
    };
    if has_wal && !shm.is_file() { return Err("store has a WAL without its shared-memory sidecar; run deploy on this stopped deployment to recover it before inspection".into()); }
    // Immutable SQLite omits WAL reads: use it only for the checkpointed main
    // image. Existing WAL+SHM can be read normally under the same exclusion.
    uri.push_str(if has_wal { "?mode=ro" } else { "?mode=ro&immutable=1" });
    let conn = Connection::open_with_flags(uri, OpenFlags::SQLITE_OPEN_READ_ONLY | OpenFlags::SQLITE_OPEN_URI).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?;
    let release: Option<String> = conn.query_row("SELECT value FROM _meta WHERE key='release'", [], |row|row.get(0)).optional().map_err(|e|e.to_string())?;
    let release = match release {
        Some(value) => value,
        None => std::fs::read_to_string(dir.join("current")).map_err(|_| missing())?,
    };
    release.trim().parse::<u64>().ok().filter(|n|*n>0).ok_or_else(missing)
}

fn pointer_current(dir: &Path, database: &Path) -> Result<u64, String> {
    let missing = || format!("no current release in {}; deploy first", dir.display());
    let pointer = std::fs::read_to_string(dir.join("current")).map_err(|_| missing())?;
    let pointer = pointer.trim().parse::<u64>().ok().filter(|n|*n>0).ok_or_else(missing)?;
    bound_at(dir.join("releases").join(pointer.to_string()))?;
    let store = snapback4_core::Store::open_read_only(database).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?;
    match store.meta("release").map_err(|e|e.to_string())? {
        Some(value) => value.parse::<u64>().ok().filter(|n|*n>0).ok_or_else(missing),
        None => Ok(pointer),
    }
}

  floor(os.availableParallelism() / 3)))` slots (3 on 10 CPUs, 24 on 96).
  This counts Linux CPU affinity and leaves room for each file's servers,
  browser renderers and workers. Acquisition and server-e2e share that pool;
  there are no extra shard processes outside it. Real-timer files listed in
  `TIMER_CLIENT_FILES` drain that pool and run individually afterward with
  unchanged timing bounds. The quote-jump browser witness remains pooled.
  The two full-count model

  still forces one client file at a time. The external file watchdog measures
  time since a subtest started or finished: max(900 seconds, largest single
  declared guard + max(60 seconds, 25%)). There is no absolute duration cap.
  Console output does not count as progress. Serial mode uses the same bounds.
  See `scripts/run-client-tests.mjs` and the QF round-2 guard census; update
  named work counts and recheck the census when declarations change. Every
  file runs as native `node --test` with default isolation, in a process group
  whose first member is a tiny Node keeper. The runner closes the keeper's stdin
  on completion, stall or abort; only the keeper calls `kill(0, SIGKILL)`.
  Runner death also closes that pipe. An unexpectedly exited keeper fails the
  file and never authorizes a runner signal. Cleanup requires two consecutive
  completed inspections (50 ms apart) with no living group member. Observed leftovers
  fail as TEST_FILE_LEAK. Failed listings and unclassifiable PID/PPID/PGID
  prefixes remain fail-closed. A ps timeout at two seconds retries once with a
  60-second bound; all other command errors refuse immediately. Listings use
  asynchronous spawn so other files' timers and keeper EOF delivery keep running.
  File watchdogs and cleanup's five-second window use per-file monotonic time.
  Expiry requires another completed snapshot: a live group after the deadline
  refuses; two consecutive empty snapshots pass even when the second is late.
  Precisely
  recognized exit-time routing anomalies
  discard the whole snapshot and retry at most twice, 50 ms apart; persistent
  anomalies refuse and never confer ownership (LLP 3000 §7, QF2).
  Metadata is validated for group members and
  their visible descendants; unrelated rows only increment diagnostics.
  Signed Darwin UIDs, variable lstart spacing and comm spaces are accepted.
  Vanished members count as exited. Processes leaving the group (setsid,

  receive metadata validation but are never signalled by the runner. Native Node's exit
  status alone decides test success; reporter counts are diagnostic only.
  The reporter observes progress as Node delivers it: synchronous work that

Do not reshape `contract.ts` from a lane; propose the change in the journal.

- Named lane: `~/projects/snapback-sb4` on `sb4/*` branches. Never `git stash`; commit checkpoints.

Bump `version` in `packages/snapback4/package.json` (and its `optionalDependencies`), `packages/snapback4-darwin-arm64/package.json`,
  `packages/snapback4-linux-x64/package.json` and `Cargo.toml` together; a test refuses a mismatch.

  suite qualification, requalify those exact bytes through the installed-consumer
  proof, and finish missing registry artifacts; an existing version must match
  its staged integrity. This also applies after `--dry-run`. An ordinary invocation
  refuses an existing stage so changed source cannot silently publish old bytes.
  Bump the version to build a new pair; preserve a partially published stage.
- Cross-host publication (LLP 3000.021 §3.3): prepare the common client once
  from clean release source, qualify that tarball on Linux, and import the
  Linux tarball plus its receipt on the Mac. Source commit, client SRI and
  Linux SRI must agree. Linux prefers working Docker/Podman, otherwise uses checksum-pinned proot
  with clean Ubuntu 24.04/Alpine root filesystems in a user cache, and refuses
  `NPM_TOKEN` or `NODE_AUTH_TOKEN`, even when empty. It never signs or packs
  the client. The Mac never rebuilds or executes Linux; `--resume` rechecks
  its copied receipt. No receipt packs a Darwin-only client; an existing
  two-platform preparation is refused on that path. Packed dependencies must
  match the staged set. Uploads use verified private snapshots, with each
  digest checked again immediately before publish.
  Commands and current qualification limits:
  [`reports/20260927-lx-linux-package/REPORT.md`](./reports/20260927-lx-linux-package/REPORT.md).
- The guide is tested text. Every ```quarry block in `crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide.rs` and in `packages/snapback4/README.md` is compiled by `crates/snapback4-lang/tests/guide.rs` with no diagnostic and no note (the language card is also run against the engine); every refusal code the sources emit must be in `guide::CODES`; every `guide("..")` a diagnostic carries must name a card. Add a form to the language card when you add it to the parser, fence README Quarry as ```quarry, and never fence Quarry as plain text to dodge the test.
- Native jobs (LLP 3000.005) need `rustup target add wasm32-unknown-unknown`; the suite's fixture build fails loudly without it. The guest SDK `crates/snapback4-native` is a path dependency until it is published; an app's `snapback/native/Cargo.toml` names it by path.

Quarry store testing (LLP 3000.035 §5.1): `SNAPBACK4_EVAL_POSTGRES` is an
explicit evaluation connection. The factory owns one physical schema per worker,
restores its rows and bookkeeping, installs the compiled fixture, then opens the
ordinary engine. Mutation attempts restore a seeded snapshot; shrinking reuses
that same factory. `Database::connect_fresh()` is an opt-in test mode. Fresh and
restored outcomes must serialize to identical bytes. The local wire observer
retains SQLSTATE, message, detail and SQL statement before the public query API
converts a driver error to a refusal; it never changes the traffic.

Reads sweep every public boundary. Mutations compare every discovered position
twice on SQLite, and compare up to eight seeded positions per dimension on
Postgres (including first/last), plus the case's original budget. Sampling
counters are in `store_stats`; each selected position runs prefetch on and off.
Physical prefetch counters are separate from the exact public outcome record.
The widened generator (`src/widen.rs`) emits prefetch-reachable reads: pages
projecting `tags[.tag]` and `tags[name = .label]` (a `Get` and a unique
`Lookup` whose keys vary across rows) and a loop reading `tags[row.tag]`, so
`store_stats` reports marked reads, transferred rows, cases with a transfer
and `prefetch_consumption_witness_probes` (fewer database calls with prefetch
on: cached answers were consumed). A planted cached-hit-to-miss fault is caught
by the random corpus (LLP 3000.035 §5.1; the closed
[coverage ticket](../issues/closed/20261007-quarry-store-oracle-prefetch-coverage-gap.md)).
